name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I wake up with this heavy, sad feeling sitting on me, and even in my bright kitchen with coffee going, I feel like the warmth has gone out of everything.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to get excited about quilting squares, bowling night, and planning potlucks, but now I look at my supplies or the church calendar and just can't make myself care the way I used to.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I came in because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, where some days I pick at toast and coffee and other days I stand in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy.\n- key: S028\n  name: grocery store or take forever to answer a simple question at meetings.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n spell after my aunt moved in, I never heard voices in the kitchen or believed the whole town was judging me like this. It’s made caregiving and staying involved in town feel much harder than before.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about seven months, since my aunt came to live with me after her fall.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 50 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you couldn't stop?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like I couldn’t stop. I might feel kind of disappointed in myself if I’ve been eating junk, but I wouldn’t say I usually feel guilty after eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating on the days you buy a lot of sweets?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I feel totally out of control, no. It’s more like my eating is just turned around lately. Some days I barely want anything, like toast and coffee, and then other days I end up standing there buying a bunch of sweets I don’t even really want.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your eating changes in response to how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. When I’m feeling worse, my eating gets really off. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even enjoy much. It’s been like that for months.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mood influences what you eat on a given day?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think it does. When my mood is worse, my eating gets really turned around. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It’s been going on for months now.\n\nDoctor: Are you eating sweets or other foods in an attempt to improve your mood on the days you're feeling worse?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it’s to improve my mood, exactly. It’s more that my eating has been all turned around for months. Some days I’m barely picking at toast and coffee, and other days I end up standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating sweets or other foods helps you feel better when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I buy the sweets sometimes, but I don’t feel better after. My eating has just been all over the place for months—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and other days I’m in the bakery aisle getting things I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Are you using food as a way to deal with your emotions when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing it on purpose to deal with feelings, no. It’s more that my eating has been messed up for months. It’s been like that for months.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mood influences what you eat on a given day?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think it does. When my mood is worse, my eating gets really turned around. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It’s been going on for months now.\n\nDoctor: Are you eating sweets or other foods in an attempt to improve your mood on the days you're feeling worse?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it’s to improve my mood, exactly. It’s more that my eating has been all turned around for months. Some days I’m barely picking at toast and coffee, and other days I end up standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating sweets or other foods helps you feel better when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I buy the sweets sometimes, but I don’t feel better after. My eating has just been all over the place for months—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and other days I’m in the bakery aisle getting things I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Are you using food as a way to deal with your emotions when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing it on purpose to deal with feelings, no. It’s more that my eating has been messed up for months. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I end up buying sweets in the bakery aisle that I don’t even really want.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself not being interested in food when you're not eating much?\n\nPatient: Yes, on those days I’m not really interested in food. I’ll just pick at something like toast and coffee and not want much else. It’s been like that for months, sort of back and forth with the days where I end up buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost your appetite on the days you're barely eating?\n\nPatient: Yes, on those days it does feel like I’ve lost my appetite. I’ll just have toast and coffee and kind of pick at it. It’s been going on for months, back and forth with the days where I end up buying sweets I don’t really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food on some days is related to your emotional state or is it just a physical feeling of not being hungry?\n\nPatient: I think it’s tied up with how I’m feeling, at least some. My eating has been all turned around for months now—some days I can barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Some days I’m barely picking at toast and coffee, and other days I end up standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating sweets or other foods helps you feel better when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I buy the sweets sometimes, but I don’t feel better after. My eating has just been all over the place for months—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and other days I’m in the bakery aisle getting things I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Are you using food as a way to deal with your emotions when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing it on purpose to deal with feelings, no. It’s more that my eating has been messed up for months. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I end up buying sweets in the bakery aisle that I don’t even really want.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself not being interested in food when you're not eating much?\n\nPatient: Yes, on those days I’m not really interested in food. I’ll just pick at something like toast and coffee and not want much else. It’s been like that for months, sort of back and forth with the days where I end up buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost your appetite on the days you're barely eating?\n\nPatient: Yes, on those days it does feel like I’ve lost my appetite. I’ll just have toast and coffee and kind of pick at it. It’s been going on for months, back and forth with the days where I end up buying sweets I don’t really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food on some days is related to your emotional state or is it just a physical feeling of not being hungry?\n\nPatient: I think it’s tied up with how I’m feeling, at least some. My eating has been all turned around for months now—some days I can barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of appetite on certain days more because you're not feeling hungry physically or because you're just not interested in eating due to how you're feeling emotionally?\n\nPatient: It feels more connected to how I’m feeling than just being physically hungry or not. Some days I barely pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It’s just gotten all turned around.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s been a big weight loss exactly. My eating has just been really uneven for months—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and other days I’m buying sweets I don’t even really want.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don’t think it’s about being afraid of gaining weight. My eating just feels kind of all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you couldn't stop?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like I couldn’t stop. It’s more that my eating has been all turned around for months—some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after eating?\n\nPatient: Not usually, no. I might feel kind of disappointed in myself if I’ve been eating junk, but I wouldn’t say I usually feel guilty after eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating on the days you buy a lot of sweets?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I feel totally out of control, no. It’s more like my eating is just turned around lately. Some days I barely want anything, like toast and coffee, and then other days I end up standing there buying a bunch of sweets I don’t even really want.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your eating changes in response to how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. When I’m feeling worse, my eating gets really off. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and then other days I end up in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even enjoy much. It’s been like that for months.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mood influences what you eat on a given day?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think it does. When my mood is worse, my eating gets really turned around. Some days I’m just picking at toast and coffee, and other days I’m in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy. It’s been going on for months now.\n\nDoctor: Are you eating sweets or other foods in an attempt to improve your mood on the days you're feeling worse?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think it’s to improve my mood, exactly. It’s more that my eating has been all turned around for months. Some days I’m barely picking at toast and coffee, and other days I end up standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think eating sweets or other foods helps you feel better when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I buy the sweets sometimes, but I don’t feel better after. My eating has just been all over the place for months—some days it’s just toast and coffee, and other days I’m in the bakery aisle getting things I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Are you using food as a way to deal with your emotions when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m doing it on purpose to deal with feelings, no. It’s more that my eating has been messed up for months. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I end up buying sweets in the bakery aisle that I don’t even really want.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself not being interested in food when you're not eating much?\n\nPatient: Yes, on those days I’m not really interested in food. I’ll just pick at something like toast and coffee and not want much else. It’s been like that for months, sort of back and forth with the days where I end up buying sweets I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost your appetite on the days you're barely eating?\n\nPatient: Yes, on those days it does feel like I’ve lost my appetite. I’ll just have toast and coffee and kind of pick at it. It’s been going on for months, back and forth with the days where I end up buying sweets I don’t really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food on some days is related to your emotional state or is it just a physical feeling of not being hungry?\n\nPatient: I think it’s tied up with how I’m feeling, at least some. My eating’s been all turned around for months—some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I’m buying sweets in the bakery aisle that I don’t even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel hungry at all on the days you're barely eating?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. On those days I’m not thinking much about food—I’ll just pick at toast and coffee.
